a.set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n    fig.suptitle('Processing steps: %s  (outputs: lickdir %s, context %s, outcome %s)'\n                 % (sessname, np.bincount(lick_dir, minlength=3),\n                    np.bincount(context, minlength=2), np.bincount(outcome, minlength=3)))\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    fig.savefig('processing_%s.png' % sessname, dpi=110)\n    plt.close(fig)\n\n    # second figure: event-time sanity (sample / delay / go cue) and trial-average traces\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(2, 1, figsize=(10, 8))\n    smp = ev['sample'][kt] - align[kt]\n    dly = ev['delay'][kt] - align[kt]\n    ax[0].plot(smp, np.arange(kt.size), 'g.', ms=3, label='sample onset')\n    ax[0].plot(dly, np.arange(kt.size), 'b.', ms=3, label='delay onset')\n    ax[0].axvline(0, color='r', label='go cue (alignment)')\n    ax[0].set_xlim(TMIN, TMAX); ax[0].legend(); ax[0].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n    ax[0].set_ylabel('trial'); ax[0].set_title('event times relative to alignment event')\n    ax[1].plot(TOUT, np.nanmean(me_ds, axis=0), 'k', label='mean motion energy')\n    ax[1].plot(TOUT, 100 * np.mean(tongue_cls != 2, axis=0), 'g', label='% trials tongue visible')\n    ax[1].axvline(0, color='r'); ax[1].legend(); ax[1].set_xlabel('time from go cue (s)')\n    ax[1].set_title('trial-averaged movement signals')\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    fig.savefig('processing_%s_alignment.png' % sessname, dpi=110)\n    plt.close(fig)\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Main\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n    ap.add_argument('outfile')\n    ap.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True)\n    ap.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true')\n    ap.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true')\n    ap.add_argument('--nproc', type=int, default=12)\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    sessions = SESSIONS[:2] if args.sample else SESSIONS\n    show = args.show_processing\n    jobs = [(a, d, p, dd, tk, show and i < 2) for i, (a, d, p, dd, tk) in enumerate(sessions)]\n\n    print('Converting %d sessions (%s mode)' % (len(jobs), 'sample' if args.sample else 'full'))\n    t0 = time.time()\n    if args.nproc > 1 and len(jobs) > 1:\n        with Pool(min(args.nproc, len(jobs))) as pool:\n            results = pool.map(process_session, jobs)\n    else:\n        results = [process_session(j) for j in jobs]\n    print('All sessions processed in %.1f s' % (time.time() - t0))\n\n    data = dict(neural=[], input=[], output=[], subjects=[], subject_idx=[],\n                brain_regions=['ALM', 'tjM1'], brain_region_idx=[])\n    session_info = []\n    for r in results:\n        if not r.get('ok'):\n            print('  EXCLUDED %s: %s' % (r['sess'], r['reason']))\n            continue\n        info = r['info']\n        print('  %s (%s): %d units, %d/%d trials, load %.1fs neural %.1fs video %.1fs total %.1fs'\n              % (info['sess'], info['task'], info['nunits'], info['ntrials_kept'],\n                 info['ntrials_total'], info['timing']['load'], info['timing']['neural'],\n                 info['timing']['video'], info['timing']['total']))\n        if r['anm'] not in data['subjects']:\n            data['subjects'].append(r['anm'])\n        data['subject_idx'].append(data['subjects'].index(r['anm']))\n        data['neural'].append(r['neural'])\n        data['input'].append(r['input'])\n        data['output'].append(r['output'])\n        data['brain_region_idx'].append(\n            np.array([data['brain_regions'].index(x) for x in r['regions']], dtype=np.int64))\n        session_info.append(info)\n\n    data['subject_idx'] = np.array(data['subject_idx'], dtype=np.int64)\n    data['input_names'] = ['time_from_go_cue']\n    data['output_names'] = ['lick_direction', 'context', 'outcome',\n                            'tongue_velocity', 'paw_velocity', 'motion_energy']\n    data['output_values'] = [\n        ['left', 'right', 'none'],\n        ['WC', 'DR'],\n        ['incorrect', 'correct', 'ignore'],\n        ['below_median', 'above_median', 'not_visible'],\n        ['below_median', 'above_median', 'not_visible'],\n        ['below_median', 'above_median', 'no_video'],\n    ]\n    data['metadata'] = dict(\n        task_description=(\n            'Head-fixed mice alternate block-wise between a delayed-response (DR) task '\n            '(auditory sample tone instructs left/right lick direction, delay epoch, auditory go cue) '\n            'and a water-cued (WC) task (water delivered at a random time at a random port, no auditory cues). '\n            'Neural activity is single-unit spiking recorded with Neuropixels/H2 probes in ALM (and tjM1 on a '\n            'few dual-probe sessions). Decoder outputs: lick direction (left/right/none), behavioral context '\n            '(WC/DR), trial outcome (incorrect/correct/ignore), and median-split discretized tongue speed, '\n            'paw speed and motion energy (class 2 = feature not visible / no video).'),\n        time_bin_size=DT_OUT * 1000.0,\n        temporal_alignment_event='go cue onset (auditory go cue on DR trials, water drop on WC trials)',\n        off_start=TMIN,\n        off_end=TMIN + NOUT * DT_OUT,\n        dataset='Hasnain, Birnbaum et al., Nature Neuroscience 2024 - '\n                '\"Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\"',\n        neural_processing=('spikes aligned to the go cue, binned at %d ms, converted to spikes/s and smoothed '\n                           'with a causal Gaussian kernel (mySmooth, N=%d), then averaged into %d ms bins'\n                           % (DT_FINE * 1000, SMOOTH_N, DT_OUT * 1000)),\n        neuron_curation=('cluster quality not in %s (findClusters.m) and mean firing rate > %g Hz '\n                         '(removeLowFRClusters.m); sessions require >= %d units'\n                         % (sorted(BAD_QUALITY), LOW_FR, MIN_UNITS)),\n        trial_curation='photostimulation trials (stim.enable) and early-lick trials removed; '\n                       'ignore (no-response) trials retained as an output class',\n        video='DeepLabCut tracking at 400 Hz aligned with findVideoOffset.m; motion energy from '\n              'motionEnergy_*.mat interpolated onto the same time axis',\n        discretization='tongue/paw speed and motion energy split at the per-session 50th percentile of '\n                       'visible values; Neuron curation = quality labels + low-FR threshold (see above).\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    80→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    81→\n    82→### Data Structure\n    83→`/app/data` contains 4 directories. Only the two ephys directories contain neural data:\n    84→\n    85→| Directory | Contents | Usable for neural decoding |\n    86→|---|---|---|\n    87→| `Ephys_Behavior/` | 25 `data_structure_ANM_DATE.mat` + 25 `motionEnergy_ANM_DATE.mat` | YES - fixed (0.9 s) delay DR task with interleaved WC blocks |\n    88→| `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/` | 22 `data_structure_*.mat` + 20 `motionEnergy_*.mat` | YES - randomized-delay DR task (2 sessions have no sorted units) |\n    89→| `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/` | 53 `data_structure_MAH*.mat` | NO - behaviour + optogenetics only, no ephys |\n    90→| `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/` | `data_structure_MAH*.mat` | NO - behaviour only |\n    91→\n    92→File formats are mixed: most `data_structure` files are MATLAB **v7.3** (HDF5, read with `h5py`); 11 are **v7** (read with `scipy.io.loadmat`). All `motionEnergy_*.mat` are v7. My loader `matio.py` handles both and returns an identical dict.\n    93→\n    94→### `obj` fields (per session)\n    95→- `obj.bp` - Bpod/trial data: `Ntrials`, `L`,`R` (instructed lick direction), `hit`,`miss`,`no` (outcome: correct/incorrect/ignore), `early` (early lick), `autowater` (1 = water-cued/WC trial, 0 = delayed-response/DR), `stim.enable` (optogenetic trial), `protocol`.\n    96→  - `obj.bp.ev` - event times **relative to trial start (bpod clock, s)**: `bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue` (= water-drop time on WC trials), `reward`, and cell arrays `lickL`, `lickR` (lickport contact times).\n    97→- `obj.clu` - cell array (1 x nProbes); each probe is a struct array of clusters with `tm` (session time), `trialtm` (time within trial), `trial` (trial number, 1-based), `quality`, `spkWavs`, `channel/site`.\n    98→- `obj.traj` - cell array (2x1): `{1}` = side cam, `{2}` = bottom cam. Each is a struct array over trials with `ts` (nframes, [x,y,confidence], nfeat), `frameTimes` (s in video clock), `featNames`, `NdroppedFrames`.\n    99→  - side-cam features: tongue, left_tongue, right_tongue, jaw, trident, nose, lickport\n   100→  - bottom-cam features: top_tongue, topleft_tongue, bottom_tongue, bottomleft_tongue, **top_paw, bottom_paw**, lickport, jaw, top_nostril, bottom_nostril\n   101→- `obj.sglx` - `fs` (25 kHz), `bitcode.bitstart` (sample index of trial-start bitcode) -> used for the video/ephys clock offset (`vidshift` ~0.49-0.5 s).\n   102→- `obj.ex.probe.loc` - probe location strings, e.g. `'R ALM'`, `'L ALM'`, `'R M1TJ'`, `'L M1TJ'`, `'DUMMY'` (not present in every file).\n   103→- `obj.trials.bp.haveEphys` / `haveVid` - per-trial flags for valid ephys/video.\n   104→- `motionEnergy_*.mat` -> `me.data` (cell, one (1 x nframes) vector per trial at 400 Hz) and `me.moveThresh` (per-session movement threshold).\n   105→\n   106→### Dataset Size (from data files, all 47 ephys sessions)\n   107→| Statistic | Value |\n   108→|-----------|-------|\n   109→| Sessions with ephys files | 47 (25 fixed-delay + 22 randomized-delay) |\n   110→| Sessions with sorted units | 45 (JEB24_2023-10-03 / 10-04 have no `obj.clu`) |\n   111→| Subjects (ephys) | 16 (EKH1, EKH3, JEB6, JEB7, JEB11, JEB12, JEB13, JEB14, JEB15, JEB19, JEB23, JEB24, JGR2, JGR3 ...) |\n   112→| Trials (total, all 47) | 15,843 |\n   113→| Trials / session | 218-517 (mean ~337) |\n   114→| Sorted clusters (all probes) | 14,297 |\n   115→| Clusters passing quality filter (not garbage/noisy) | 3,363 |\n   116→| Cluster quality labels | garbage 10931, multi 1136, fair 784, poor 664, good 304, (empty) 206, great 192, excellent 74, mutli 2, ood 1, gabrga 1, real? 1, noisy 1 |\n   117→\n   118→Notes:\n   119→- Quality strings are capitalized in the files (`Poor`, `Great`, ...); the reference `findClusters` compares to lowercase names, so I match **case-insensitively** (same intent).\n   120→- Sessions recorded with 2 probes have one ALM probe and one tjM1 probe (except JEB15 which has some bilateral ALM sessions); the reference meta loaders specify which probe is ALM.\n   121→- Autowater (WC) trials exist in all fixed-delay sessions (aw = 0-122 per session) but are essentially absent in the randomized-delay sessions (aw = 0-39, often 0).\n   122→\n   123→---\n   124→\n   125→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   126→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   127→\n   128→### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n   129→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   130→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   131→| DR-task units | 1,651 units (483 single units) in ALM, 25 sessions, 9 mice | \"For the DR task, we recorded 1,651 units (483 single units) in ALM from 25 sessions using nine mice.\" |\n   132→| Two-context (DR+WC) sessions | 12 sessions, 6 mice, 522 units (214 SU) | \"In 12 sessions from six mice, animals performed the two-context task. In total, 522 units (214 well-isolated single units) were recorded in these sessions.\" |\n   133→| Randomized-delay units | 845 units (288 SU) in ALM, 19 sessions, 4 mice | \"for the randomized delay task, we recorded 845 units (288 well-isolated single units) in ALM from 19 sessions using four mice.\" |\n   134→| Session inclusion (ephys) | >= 10 units | \"Recording sessions were included for analysis only if they had at least 10 units\" |\n   135→| Unit inclusion | firing rate > 1 Hz | \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included in all other analyses.\" |\n   136→| Behavioural session inclusion | >= 40 correct DR trials per direction and >= 20 correct WC trials per direction | \"All sessions used for behavioral analysis had at least 40 correct DR trials for each direction (left or right) and 20 correct WC trials for each direction, excluding early lick and ignore trials\" |\n   137→| Early-lick / ignore trials | omitted from analyses | same quote; and \"Trials in which the animal contacted the lickport before the reward ('early lick') were omitted from analyses.\" |\n   138→| Sample tone | 1.3 s | \"one of two auditory tones lasting 1.3 s was played\" |\n   139→| Delay epoch | 0.9 s (0.7 s for one mouse, time-warped to 0.9 s) | \"The delay epoch (0.9 s for 12 mice, 0.7 s for one mouse ...)\" |\n   140→| Randomized delay durations | 0.3, 0.6, 1.2, 1.8, 2.4, 3.6 s (exponential pdf weighting) | \"randomly selected from six possible values (0.3 s, 0.6 s, 1.2 s, 1.8 s, 2.4 s and 3.6 s)\" |\n   141→| Go cue | 10 ms chirp | \"an auditory go cue was played (swept-frequency cosine (chirp), 10 ms)\" |\n   142→| Ignore trial | no response within 3 s of go cue | \"If the animal did not respond within 3 s of the go cue, this was considered an 'ignore' trial\" |\n   143→| Block length | 10-25 trials, sessions start with DR | \"Each interleaved block was 10-25 trials... All sessions started with DR trials.\" |\n   144→| ITI | exponential, mean 1.5 s | \"ITIs were randomly drawn from an exponential distribution with mean 1.5 s.\" |\n   145→| Video | 2 cameras (side, bottom) at 400 Hz, DeepLabCut | \"High-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate) from two cameras\" |\n   146→| Kinematics | x/y position from DLC, missing values filled with nearest **except tongue**; velocity = first derivative of position | \"Missing values were filled in with the nearest available value for all features, except for the tongue. The velocity of each feature was then calculated as the first-order derivative of the position vector.\" |\n   147→| Paws | tracked with bottom view only | \"The tongue, jaw and nose were tracked using both cameras, whereas the paws were tracked using only the bottom view.\" |\n   148→| Motion energy | 99th-percentile of frame-wise |median(next 5 frames) - median(prev 5 frames)|; per-session manual movement threshold | \"Motion energy for each frame was then converted to a single value by taking the 99th percentile ... A threshold above which an animal was classed as moving was defined on a per-session basis manually.\" |\n   149→| Optogenetics | ~30% of trials in photoinactivation sessions | \"Optogenetic photoinactivation was deployed on approximately 30% of trials selected at random.\" |\n   150→| Choice decoding from CDchoice | AUC 0.86 +/- 0.11 | \"Animal's upcoming choice could be reliably decoded from projections along CDchoice across sessions (AUC: 0.86 +/- 0.11)\" |\n   151→\n   152→### Processing Details (paper + reference code)\n   153→- **Temporal alignment**: all analyses align to the **go cue** (= water-drop time on WC trials), `params.alignEvent='goCue'`, window **-2.5 s to +2.5 s**.\n   154→- **Temporal binning**: figure scripts use `params.dt = 3/100 = 30 ms`; spikes are binned with `histc` and smoothed with a **causal** Gaussian kernel (`params.smooth = 15` bins) producing firing rates (spikes/s). Decoding analyses further aggregate into 75 ms bins.\n   155→- **Video/ephys sync**: subtract `vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs - mode(bp.ev.bitStart)` (~0.49 s, the 0.5 s pad) from `traj.frameTimes` before aligning to the go cue.\n   156→- **Motion energy** is aligned identically and NaN-filled with 'nearest'.\n   157→\n   158→### Curation Steps\n   159→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   160→1. Drop clusters whose quality is `garbage`/`gabrga`/`noisy`/`real?` (`findClusters`, case-insensitive here).\n   161→2. Drop clusters with mean firing rate <= 1 Hz (`removeLowFRClusters`, `params.lowFR=1`), matching \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included\".\n   162→3. Only ALM probes are used (`meta.probe` in the loader scripts; `obj.ex.probe.loc`).\n   163→\n   164→**Trial curation rules**:\n   165→1. All paper analyses use `~stim.enable` (no optogenetic stimulation) and `~early` (no early lick).\n   166→2. Ignore (`no`) trials are omitted from the paper's behavioural analyses but are a required decoder output class here (\"ignore\"), so they are retained (see Step 5).\n   167→3. All `motionEnergy_*.mat` are v7. My loader `matio.py` handles both and returns an identical dict.\n\n### `obj` fields (per session)\n- `obj.bp` - Bpod/trial data: `Ntrials`, `L`,`R` (instructed lick direction), `hit`,`miss`,`no` (outcome: correct/incorrect/ignore), `early` (early lick), `autowater` (1 = water-cued/WC trial, 0 = delayed-response/DR), `stim.enable` (optogenetic trial), `protocol`.\n  - `obj.bp.ev` - event times **relative to trial start (bpod clock, s)**: `bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue` (= water-drop time on WC trials), `reward`, and cell arrays `lickL`, `lickR` (lickport contact times).\n- `obj.clu` - cell array (1 x nProbes); each probe is a struct array of clusters with `tm` (session time), `trialtm` (time within trial), `trial` (trial number, 1-based), `quality`, `spkWavs`, `channel/site`.\n- `obj.traj` - cell array (2x1): `{1}` = side cam, `{2}` = bottom cam. Each is a struct array over trials with `ts` (nframes, [x,y,confidence], nfeat), `frameTimes` (s in video clock), `featNames`, `NdroppedFrames`.\n  - side-cam features: tongue, left_tongue, right_tongue, jaw, trident, nose, lickport\n  - bottom-cam features: top_tongue, topleft_tongue, bottom_tongue, bottomleft_tongue, **top_paw, bottom_paw**, lickport, jaw, top_nostril, bottom_nostril\n- `obj.sglx` - `fs` (25 kHz), `bitcode.bitstart` (sample index of trial-start bitcode) -> used for the video/ephys clock offset (`vidshift` ~0.49-0.5 s).\n- `obj.ex.probe.loc` - probe location strings, e.g. `'R ALM'`, `'L ALM'`, `'R M1TJ'`, `'L M1TJ'`, `'DUMMY'` (not present in every file).\n- `obj.trials.bp.haveEphys` / `haveVid` - per-trial flags for valid ephys/video.\n- `motionEnergy_*.mat` -> `me.data` (cell, one (1 x nframes) vector per trial at 400 Hz) and `me.moveThresh` (per-session movement threshold).\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files, all 47 ephys sessions)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions with ephys files | 47 (25 fixed-delay + 22 randomized-delay) |\n| Sessions with sorted units | 45 (JEB24_2023-10-03 / 10-04 have no `obj.clu`) |\n| Subjects (ephys) | 16 (EKH1, EKH3, JEB6, JEB7, JEB11, JEB12, JEB13, JEB14, JEB15, JEB19, JEB23, JEB24, JGR2, JGR3 ...) |\n| Trials (total, all 47) | 15,843 |\n| Trials / session | 218-517 (mean ~337) |\n| Sorted clusters (all probes) | 14,297 |\n| Clusters passing quality filter (not garbage/noisy) | 3,363 |\n| Cluster quality labels | garbage 10931, multi 1136, fair 784, poor 664, good 304, (empty) 206, great 192, excellent 74, mutli 2, ood 1, gabrga 1, real? Decoding analyses further aggregate into 75 ms bins.\n- **Video/ephys sync**: subtract `vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs - mode(bp.ev.bitStart)` (~0.49 s, the 0.5 s pad) from `traj.frameTimes` before aligning to the go cue.\n- **Motion energy** is aligned identically and NaN-filled with 'nearest'.\n\n### Curation Steps\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n1. Drop clusters whose quality is `garbage`/`gabrga`/`noisy`/`real?` (`findClusters`, case-insensitive here).\n2. Drop clusters with mean firing rate <= 1 Hz (`removeLowFRClusters`, `params.lowFR=1`), matching \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included\".\n3. Only ALM probes are used (`meta.probe` in the loader scripts; `obj.ex.probe.loc`).\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n1. All paper analyses use `~stim.enable` (no optogenetic stimulation) and `~early` (no early lick).\n2. Ignore (`no`) trials are omitted from the paper's behavioural analyses but are a required decoder output class here (\"ignore\"), so they are retained (see Step 5).\n3. Sessions require >40 right-hit and >40 left-hit DR trials (`UseInclusionCritera`) and >=10 units.\n\n### Decoders Trained (reference paper)\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| Choice (L/R) from neural data, 4-fold CV | rises from ~0.5 pre-sample to ~0.9-1.0 after go cue (Fig. 3b) |\n| Choice from kinematics | similar, ~0.85-0.95 after go cue |\n| Context (DR/WC) from neural data | ~0.8-1.0 across the trial, above chance even in the ITI (Fig. 4b) |\n| Context from kinematics | ~0.8-0.95 (Fig. 4b) |\n| CDchoice ROC-AUC | 0.86 +/- 0.11 |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Sessions used | meta loaders list 44 ephys sessions (25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay). `JEB23_2023-10-20` is commented out; `JEB4`,`JEB5` loaders exist but those data are not distributed | 47 `data_structure` files; `JEB24_2023-10-03/04` have **no `obj.clu`** (no sorted units, and no motionEnergy file); `JEB23_2023-10-19` and `10-20` have identical trial counts/statistics (duplicate) | \"25 sessions\" (DR) and \"19 sessions\" (randomized delay) | Use exactly the 44 sessions listed (uncommented) in the meta loaders = 25 + 19, matching the paper. `(L&hit)|(R&miss)` -> left(0); `no` -> none(2); constant over time | `getPrevChoice` | verified == direction of first lick after go cue |\n   220→| `bp.autowater` | `output[1]` = `context` | 1 -> WC(0), 0 -> DR(1) | `WorkingWithDataObjs.m`, condition strings `autowater` | constant over time |\n   221→| `bp.hit`,`bp.miss`,`bp.no` | `output[2]` = `outcome` | miss -> incorrect(0), hit -> correct(1), no -> ignore(2) | `getOutcome` | constant over time |\n   222→| `obj.traj{1}` feature `tongue` (side cam) | `output[3]` = `tongue_velocity` | interp x/y onto the 10 ms grid using `frameTimes - vidshift - goCue`; velocity = `gradient` of x and y (no NaN filling for tongue); speed = hypot(vx,vy); average over visible samples in each 50 ms bin; per-session median over visible bins -> 0 (<50th pct) / 1 (>=50th pct); **not visible -> 2** | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematicsFromVideo` | tongue is NaN (not labelled by DLC) ~93% of frames = not visible |\n   223→| `obj.traj{2}` features `top_paw`,`bottom_paw` (bottom cam) | `output[4]` = `paw_velocity` | same pipeline, speed averaged over the two paw markers; visible only where DLC labelled the paw in that bin; 50th-percentile split; not visible -> 2 | same | paws only tracked in bottom view (paper) |\n   224→| `motionEnergy_ANM_DATE.mat` `me.data` | `output[5]` = `motion_energy` | interp 400 Hz ME onto the 10 ms grid using `frameTimes - vidshift - goCue`, nearest-fill; average into 50 ms bins; per-session median -> 0/1; **no video -> 2** | `loadMotionEnergy` | |\n   225→\n   226→### Key Decisions\n   227→1. **Alignment to go cue** (`params.alignEvent='goCue'`) and window **[-2.5, +2.5] s** - exactly the reference `params.tmin/tmax`. On WC trials `bp.ev.goCue` is the water-drop time, which is what the paper uses (\"Time from go cue/water drop\").\n   228→2. **Binning**: reference bins at 10 ms and smooths with a causal Gaussian (15 bins) before decoding, then averages into 75 ms decoding bins. I keep the reference's 10 ms binning + smoothing and average into **50 ms** bins: 100 timepoints/trial, a compromise between the reference decoding bin (75 ms) and temporal resolution for time-varying outputs, and it puts the go cue exactly on a bin edge.\n   229→3. **Firing rates rather than spike counts**: the reference `trialdat` (spikes/s, smoothed) is what is fed to all reference decoders.\n   230→4. **Neuron curation** = reference (`findClusters` quality filter + `removeLowFRClusters` at 1 Hz), matching \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included in all other analyses\".\n   231→5. **Trial curation** = reference (`~stim.enable & ~early`), but ignore trials are retained because they are an explicit output class.\n   232→6. **Both task variants included** (fixed and randomized delay) to use all available neural data; randomized-delay sessions contribute few/no WC trials, which is a property of the experiment, not a conversion error. `metadata['session_info']` records the task type.\n   233→7. **Discretization thresholds** are computed **per session** on the pooled (trial x bin) distribution of *visible* values, as specified by the task (50th percentile).\n   234→8. **Video/ephys synchronisation** uses the reference `findVideoOffset` (vidshift ~0.49 s) with the reference fallback (`(1:n)/400 - 0.5`).\n   235→9. **Not-visible classes**: tongue/paw bins with no DLC label, and ME on trials with no usable video, get class 2 (as specified).\n   236→\n   237→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   238→- [ ] Session count == 44 (25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay), animals == 14.\n   239→- [ ] Total kept ALM units in the 25 fixed-delay sessions vs paper's 1,651 recorded units / after 1 Hz filter; randomized-delay vs 845.\n   240→- [ ] Every session has >= 10 units and >= 2 trials.\n   241→- [ ] Trial counts: kept trials == Ntrials - (stim | early) per session.\n   242→- [ ] Lick direction == direction of first lickport contact after the go cue (independent raw-data check).\n   243→- [ ] Outcome distribution: hit fraction ~60-80% (from raw `bp.hit`).\n   244→- [ ] Context: WC fraction ~30% in fixed-delay sessions, ~0 in randomized-delay.\n   245→- [ ] Input range == [-2.475, 2.475] s exactly for every trial.\n   246→- [ ] Tongue velocity class 2 fraction high before the go cue and low just after (licking occurs after the go cue).\n   247→- [ ] Motion energy / tongue / paw classes 0 and 1 each ~50% of *visible* bins (by construction of the median split).\n   248→- [ ] Spot-check: recompute the binned firing rate of a specific neuron/trial directly from the raw `.mat` file and compare with `np.allclose`.\n   249→\n   250→---\n   251→\n   252→## Step 6: Script Development\n   253→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   254→\n   255→`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan from Step 5. Structure:\n   256→\n   257→| Section | Functions | Reference counterpart |\n   258→|---|---|---|\n   259→| MATLAB IO | `is_v73`, `_load_v73`, `_load_v7`, `load_session`, `load_motion_energy` | `loadObjs.m`, `loadMotionEnergy.m` (handles both v7 and v7.3 files; only the needed fields/features are read) |\n   260→| Smoothing | `gausswin`, `causal_kernel`, `my_smooth` | `utils/mySmooth.m` (causal Gaussian, 'reflect' boundary) |\n   261→| Time axis | `time_axes` | `getSeq.m` (`edges = tmin:dt:tmax`, centres at `edges + dt/2`) |\n   262→| Neural | `bin_spikes` | `alignSpikes.m` + `getSeq.m` + `findClusters.m`; low-FR filter applied in `process_session` as in `removeLowFRClusters.m` |\n   263→| Video sync | `video_offset`, `frame_times_for_trial` | `funcs/findVideoOffset.m`, `findPosition.m` fallback |\n   264→| Kinematics | `feature_speed` | `findPosition.m` + `findVelocity.m` |\n   265→| Motion energy | `motion_energy_trials` | `loadMotionEnergy.m` |\n   266→| Discretization | `discretize` | new (decoder spec: per-session 50th percentile) |\n   267→| Session conversion | `process_session` | `processData.m` + `loadSessionData.m` |\n   268→| Plots | `plot_processing` | new (`--show-processing`) |\n   269→\n   270→Session list `SESSIONS` is transcribed from the reference meta loaders (44 sessions, with the ALM probe number(s) per session).\n   271→\n   272→Code efficiency:\n   273→- Only the needed probes/DLC features are read out of the (100-300 MB) HDF5 files, instead of loading whole `obj` structs.\n   274→- Spike binning is vectorised over spikes (`np.add.at` on a flat index) instead of per-trial `histc` loops.\n   275→- Smoothing is an FFT convolution over all trials at once.\n   276→- Sessions are processed in parallel (`--nproc`, default 12).\n   277→\n   278→Timing: 2 sample sessions took 3.9 s and 4.6 s each (dominated by file loading).\n   279→\n   280→---\n   281→\n   282→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and The converter follows the reference; the check was corrected.\n   448→- The first version of my lick-direction check ignored the paper's 3 s response window. |\n   452→|---|---|---|---|\n   453→| (a) data loading | `loadObjs.m` loads `obj`; session/probe list from `load*_ALMVideo.m` | `load_session()` reads the same fields; `SESSIONS` transcribes the loaders (44 sessions, ALM probes) | YES |\n   454→| (b) neuron filtering | `findClusters.m` (exclude garbage/gabrga/noisy/'real?') then `removeLowFRClusters.m` (mean PSTH rate > `params.lowFR` = 1 Hz) | identical, case-insensitive; mean over the same window/trials | YES (see note) |\n   455→| (b) trial filtering | condition strings `~stim.enable & ~early` | identical; ignore trials kept (required output class); plus trials with no ephys coverage dropped | YES + documented additions |\n   456→| (c) temporal alignment | `alignSpikes.m`: `trialtm - bp.ev.goCue(trial)`; video: `frameTimes - vidshift - goCue` (`findPosition.m`, `loadMotionEnergy.m`) | identical, including the `(1:n)/400 - 0.5` fallback | YES |\n   457→| (d) binning | `getSeq.m`: `edges = tmin:dt:tmax`, `histc`, drop last bin, `/dt`, `mySmooth(...,15,'reflect')` | identical at dt = 10 ms, then averaged into 50 ms decoder bins (reference decoding averages into 75 ms bins) | YES + documented change |\n   458→| (e) input construction | reference decoders use time bins implicitly | `time_from_go_cue` = bin centres | new (decoder spec) |\n   459→| (f) output construction | choice `(R&hit)|(L&miss)` (`getPrevChoice.m`); outcome `hit`, ignore -> NaN (`getOutcome.m`); context = `autowater`; kinematics `findPosition/findVelocity`; ME `loadMotionEnergy` | identical definitions, with `none`/`ignore` classes instead of NaN, and the median split required by the decoder spec | YES + documented changes |\n   460→\n   461→Note on (b): `removeLowFRClusters.m` computes the mean of the **condition PSTHs** (`obj.psth`), i.e. a mean over condition-averaged traces; I use the mean over all kept trials. Both estimate the same quantity (mean firing rate over the trial window); mine is the unweighted trial mean, which is the more natural estimate and does not depend on the arbitrary condition list. Effect: 2,513 units pass the quality filter and 2,456 (97.7%) also pass the 1 Hz filter.\n   462→\n   463→### Check 4: Key statistics comparison\n   464→See the table in Step 9. drop trials with NaN go-cue. **Ignore (`no`) trials are kept** because the decoder must output an `ignore` outcome / `none` lick direction class.\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `obj.clu{probe}(i).trialtm`, `.trial`, `.quality` | `neural` | align: `trialtm - bp.ev.goCue(trial)`; average into 50 ms bins; per-session median -> 0/1; **no video -> 2** | `loadMotionEnergy` | |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Alignment to go cue** (`params.alignEvent='goCue'`) and window **[-2.5, +2.5] s** - exactly the reference `params.tmin/tmax`. On WC trials `bp.ev.goCue` is the water-drop time, which is what the paper uses (\"Time from go cue/water drop\").\n2. **Binning**: reference bins at 10 ms and smooths with a causal Gaussian (15 bins) before decoding, then averages into 75 ms decoding bins. I keep the reference's 10 ms binning + smoothing and average into **50 ms** bins: 100 timepoints/trial, a compromise between the reference decoding bin (75 ms) and temporal resolution for time-varying outputs, and it puts the go cue exactly on a bin edge.\n3. **Firing rates rather than spike counts**: the reference `trialdat` (spikes/s, smoothed) is what is fed to all reference decoders.\n4. **Neuron curation** = reference (`findClusters` quality filter + `removeLowFRClusters` at 1 Hz), matching \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included in all other analyses\".\n5. **Trial curation** = reference (`~stim.enable & ~early`), but ignore trials are retained because they are an explicit output class.\n6. **Both task variants included** (fixed and randomized delay) to use all available neural data; randomized-delay sessions contribute few/no WC trials, which is a property of the experiment, not a conversion error. `metadata['session_info']` records the task type.\n7. **Discretization thresholds** are computed **per session** on the pooled (trial x bin) distribution of *visible* values, as specified by the task (50th percentile).\n8. **Video/ephys synchronisation** uses the reference `findVideoOffset` (vidshift ~0.49 s) with the reference fallback (`(1:n)/400 - 0.5`).\n9. **Not-visible classes**: tongue/paw bins with no DLC label, and ME on trials with no usable video, get class 2 (as specified).\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Session count == 44 (25 fixed-delay + 19 randomized-delay), animals == 14.\n- [ ] Total kept ALM units in the 25 fixed-delay sessions vs paper's 1,651 recorded units / after 1 Hz filter; randomized-delay vs 845.\n- [ ] Every session has >= 10 units and >= 2 trials.\n- [ ] Trial counts: kept trials == Ntrials - (stim | early) per session.\n- [ ] Lick direction == direction of first lickport contact after the go cue (independent raw-data check).\n- [ ] Outcome distribution: hit fraction ~60-80% (from raw `bp.hit`).\n- [ ] Context: WC fraction ~30% in fixed-delay sessions, ~0 in randomized-delay.\n- [ ] Input range == [-2.475, 2.475] s exactly for every trial.\n- [ ] Tongue velocity class 2 fraction high before the go cue and low just after (licking occurs after the go cue).\n- [ ] Motion energy / tongue / paw classes 0 and 1 each ~50% of *visible* bins (by construction of the median split).\n- [ ] Spot-check: recompute the binned firing rate of a specific neuron/trial directly from the raw `.mat` file and compare with `np.allclose`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan from Step 5. Three filters: (1) photostimulation trials (`stim.enable`) removed, (2) early-lick trials (`early`) removed, (3) trials with NaN go-cue alignment time removed. Additionally, after neural binning, trials where no unit fires any spike (ephys recording ended early) are dropped. Ignore trials are kept (required by the decoder spec). The AI reads only the specified ALM probes per session as defined in the SESSIONS list, matching `meta.probe` in the reference loaders.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are aligned to the go cue, binned into 10 ms bins (matching `params.dt = 1/100`) over [-2.5, 2.5] s, converted to spikes/s by dividing by dt, then smoothed with a **causal** Gaussian kernel (matching `mySmooth.m`: `gausswin(15)` with the first `floor(15/2) = 7` taps zeroed, then normalized). The smoothed rates are then averaged into 50 ms bins (5x downsampling), yielding 100 timepoints per trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef causal_kernel(N=SMOOTH_N):\n    k = gausswin(N)\n    k[:N // 2] = 0.0\n    return k / k.sum()\n\ndef my_smooth(x, kern=KERN, bctype=BCTYPE):\n    from scipy.signal import fftconvolve\n    N = len(kern)\n    if bctype == 'reflect':\n        xf = np.concatenate([x[..., :N], x], axis=-1)\n        trim = N\n    ...\n\n# In bin_spikes:\ncounts = np.zeros((nkeep, NFINE), dtype=np.float32)\n# ... spike counting via np.add.at ...\nrate = counts / DT_FINE                          # spikes / s\nrate = my_smooth(rate)                           # causal gaussian\nrates.append(downsample_mean(rate).astype(np.float32))\n```\n\niii. The AI explicitly documents matching the reference code's `getSeq.m` and `mySmooth.m`: \"10 ms binning + causal Gaussian smoothing (15 bins) exactly as `getSeq`/`mySmooth`\". The 50 ms downsampling is documented as \"a compromise between the reference decoding bin (75 ms) and temporal resolution\".\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters: (1) cluster quality labels matching {garbage, gabrga, noisy, real?} are excluded (case-insensitive comparison, matching `findClusters.m`). From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"`findClusters` excludes lowercase 'garbage','gabrga','noisy','real?'\" and \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included\" (paper quote). Session minimum of 10 units from: \"Recording sessions were included for analysis only if they had at least 10 units.\"\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Spike times (`trialtm`) are subtracted by the go cue time (`bp.ev.goCue`) for their respective trial, matching `alignSpikes.m`. This puts spikes in seconds relative to the go cue onset.\n\nii.\n```python\naligned = tm[sel] - align_times[tr[sel]]\nb = np.floor((aligned - TMIN) / DT_FINE).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"identical, including the `(1:n)/400 - 0.5` fallback\" when compared with the reference `alignSpikes.m`.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. Spikes are initially binned at 10 ms (DT_FINE = 0.01), matching `params.dt = 1/100` in the reference. After smoothing, the 500 fine bins are averaged into groups of 5 to produce 100 output bins at 50 ms resolution (DT_OUT = 0.05). This is applied consistently to neural data and all video-derived outputs.\n\nii.\n```python\nDT_FINE = 0.01              # params.dt (1/100 s)\nDOWNSAMPLE = 5              # 10 ms -> 50 ms decoder bins\nDT_OUT = DT_FINE * DOWNSAMPLE\n\ndef downsample_mean(x, k=DOWNSAMPLE, nanmean=False):\n    n = (x.shape[-1] // k) * k\n    y = x[..., :n].reshape(x.shape[:-1] + (n // k, k))\n    ...\n    return y.mean(axis=-1)\n```\n\niii. The AI explains the 50 ms bin size as \"a compromise between the reference decoding bin (75 ms) and temporal resolution for time-varying outputs, and it puts the go cue exactly on a bin edge.\"\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. This is a synthetic variable defined by the time axis. It equals the bin centres of the 50 ms output bins, spanning approximately -2.475 to +2.475 s.\n\nii.\n```python\nedges = np.round(np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + 1e-9, DT_FINE), 6)\ncentres = edges[:-1] + DT_FINE / 2.0\n...\ncentres_out = centres[:nout * DOWNSAMPLE].reshape(nout, DOWNSAMPLE).mean(axis=1)\n...\ntin = TOUT.astype(np.float32)[None, :]\ninputs = [tin.copy() for _ in range(kt.size)]\n```\n\niii. N/A - this is defined by the decoder specification.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds*?\n\ni. The bin centres are computed once from the time axis parameters. No processing of raw data is needed; this variable is the shared time grid for all trials.\n\nii. Same as 3-a above.\n\niii. N/A.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The input is the bin centres of the same grid used for neural data, so alignment is automatic by construction.\n\nii.\n```python\nEDGES, TCENT, TOUT = time_axes()\n# TOUT is used as both the input variable and defines the neural time axis\n```\n\niii. N/A.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Three per-trial fields: `bp.R` (right-instructed), `bp.hit`, and `bp.miss`. The actual lick direction is inferred from the combination of instructed side and outcome.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = np.nan_to_num(bp['hit']).astype(bool)\nmiss = np.nan_to_num(bp['miss']).astype(bool)\nR = np.nan_to_num(bp['R']).astype(bool)\nL = np.nan_to_num(bp['L']).astype(bool)\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"Verified in data: identical to the direction of the first lickport contact after the go cue on 382/382 trials of JEB6_2021-04-18.\"\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction*?\n\ni. A hit on a right-instructed trial means the animal licked right; a miss on a right-instructed trial means it licked left (and vice versa). Ignore trials get class 2 (none). Codes: left=0, right=1, none=2. The value is constant across all time bins within a trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nlick_dir = np.full(kt.size, 2, dtype=np.int64)          # 2 = none\nright_lick = (R[kt] & hit[kt]) | (L[kt] & miss[kt])\nleft_lick = (L[kt] & hit[kt]) | (R[kt] & miss[kt])\nlick_dir[right_lick] = 1\nlick_dir[left_lick] = 0\n```\n\niii. Matches `getPrevChoice.m`: `choice = (R&hit)|(L&miss)`.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Behavioral context* derived from?\n\ni. `bp.autowater`: 1 = water-cued (WC) trial, 0 = delayed-response (DR) trial.\n\nii.\n```python\naw = np.nan_to_num(bp['autowater']).astype(bool)\ncontext = np.where(aw[kt], 0, 1).astype(np.int64)       # 0 = WC, 1 = DR\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"`autowater` used 'as a proxy for obtaining water-cued blocks and delayed-response blocks' (WorkingWithDataObjs.m)\".\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Behavioral context*?\n\ni. Direct mapping: autowater=1 -> WC (0), autowater=0 -> DR (1). The value is constant across all time bins. No further processing.\n\nii. Same as 5-a above.\n\niii. Matches the reference code's condition strings.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. `bp.hit` and `bp.miss`. Ignore trials are identified as those that are neither hit nor miss.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = np.nan_to_num(bp['hit']).astype(bool)\nmiss = np.nan_to_num(bp['miss']).astype(bool)\noutcome = np.full(kt.size, 2, dtype=np.int64)           # 2 = ignore\noutcome[hit[kt]] = 1\noutcome[miss[kt]] = 0\n```\n\niii. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue velocity* derived from?\n\ni. DeepLabCut tracking from the **side camera only**: feature `tongue` in `obj.traj{1}`. The AI documents: \"The tongue, jaw and nose were tracked using both cameras, whereas the paws were tracked using only the bottom view\" (paper quote), but only extracts the side camera tongue feature. The bottom camera's `top_tongue` feature is not used for tongue velocity.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. (1) Position is interpolated from frame times onto the 10 ms fine grid using `np.interp`. (2) NaN-marked frames (DLC not visible) remain NaN (no filling, matching the reference `findPosition.m` exception for tongue). (3) Velocity is computed as `np.gradient` of the interpolated position. (4) Speed = `np.hypot(vx, vy)`. (5) Non-visible bins remain NaN. (6) Speed is downsampled to 50 ms bins using `nanmean`. (7) Discretized at the per-session 50th percentile; not-visible bins -> class 2.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef feature_speed(sess, key, keep_trials, vidshift, align_times, fill_missing):\n    ...\n    pos = np.empty((NFINE, 2))\n    for d in range(2):\n        pos[:, d] = np.interp(TCENT, ft[valid], xy[valid, d], left=np.nan, right=np.nan)\n    vis = np.isfinite(pos).all(axis=1)\n    ...\n    vel = np.gradient(pos, axis=0)\n    sp = np.hypot(vel[:, 0], vel[:, 1])\n    sp[~vis] = np.nan\n    ...\ntongue_ds = downsample_mean(tongue_sp, nanmean=True)\ntongue_cls, tongue_thr = discretize(tongue_ds)\n```\n\niii. Follows `findPosition.m` (interp1 onto time axis) and `findVelocity.m` (gradient). Tongue NaNs are preserved as in the reference. No normalization since only one camera view is used.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session 50th percentile of all finite (visible) tongue speed values across all trials and time bins. Below threshold = 0, at or above = 1, not visible (NaN) = 2.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize(values, nan_class=2):\n    finite = np.isfinite(values)\n    cls = np.full(values.shape, nan_class, dtype=np.int64)\n    if finite.any():\n        thr = np.percentile(values[finite], 50)\n        cls[finite & (values >= thr)] = 1\n        cls[finite & (values < thr)] = 0\n    ...\n    return cls, thr\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder specification: \"0: < 50th percentile, 1: >= 50th percentile, 2: not visible\".\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The smoothed rates are then averaged into 50 ms bins (5x downsampling), yielding 100 timepoints per trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef causal_kernel(N=SMOOTH_N):\n    k = gausswin(N)\n    k[:N // 2] = 0.0\n    return k / k.sum()\n\ndef my_smooth(x, kern=KERN, bctype=BCTYPE):\n    from scipy.signal import fftconvolve\n    N = len(kern)\n    if bctype == 'reflect':\n        xf = np.concatenate([x[..., :N], x], axis=-1)\n        trim = N\n    ...\n\n# In bin_spikes:\ncounts = np.zeros((nkeep, NFINE), dtype=np.float32)\n# ... spike counting via np.add.at ...\nrate = counts / DT_FINE                          # spikes / s\nrate = my_smooth(rate)                           # causal gaussian\nrates.append(downsample_mean(rate).astype(np.float32))\n```\n\niii. The AI explicitly documents matching the reference code's `getSeq.m` and `mySmooth.m`: \"10 ms binning + causal Gaussian smoothing (15 bins) exactly as `getSeq`/`mySmooth`\". The 50 ms downsampling is documented as \"a compromise between the reference decoding bin (75 ms) and temporal resolution\".\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters: (1) cluster quality labels matching {garbage, gabrga, noisy, real?} are excluded (case-insensitive comparison, matching `findClusters.m`). (2) Units whose mean firing rate (over all kept trials and the full time window) is <= 1 Hz are dropped (`removeLowFRClusters.m`, `params.lowFR = 1`). Additionally, sessions with fewer than 10 surviving units are excluded.\n\nii.\n```python\nBAD_QUALITY = {'garbage', 'gabrga', 'noisy', 'real?'}\n...\ngood = [i for i, q in enumerate(c['quality'])\n        if q.strip().lower() not in BAD_QUALITY]\n...\n# removeLowFRClusters.m:\nmean_fr = rates.mean(axis=(0, 2))\nuse = mean_fr > LOW_FR\nrates = rates[:, use, :]\n...\nif rates.shape[1] < MIN_UNITS:\n    return dict(ok=False, ...)\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"`findClusters` excludes lowercase 'garbage','gabrga','noisy','real?'\" and \"All units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included\" (paper quote). Session minimum of 10 units from: \"Recording sessions were included for analysis only if they had at least 10 units.\"\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Spike times (`trialtm`) are subtracted by the go cue time (`bp.ev.goCue`) for their respective trial, matching `alignSpikes.m`. This puts spikes in seconds relative to the go cue onset.\n\nii.\n```python\naligned = tm[sel] - align_times[tr[sel]]\nb = np.floor((aligned - TMIN) / DT_FINE).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"identical, including the `(1:n)/400 - 0.5` fallback\" when compared with the reference `alignSpikes.m`.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. Spikes are initially binned at 10 ms (DT_FINE = 0.01), matching `params.dt = 1/100` in the reference. After smoothing, the 500 fine bins are averaged into groups of 5 to produce 100 output bins at 50 ms resolution (DT_OUT = 0.05). This is applied consistently to neural data and all video-derived outputs.\n\nii.\n```python\nDT_FINE = 0.01              # params.dt (1/100 s)\nDOWNSAMPLE = 5              # 10 ms -> 50 ms decoder bins\nDT_OUT = DT_FINE * DOWNSAMPLE\n\ndef downsample_mean(x, k=DOWNSAMPLE, nanmean=False):\n    n = (x.shape[-1] // k) * k\n    y = x[..., :n].reshape(x.shape[:-1] + (n // k, k))\n    ...\n    return y.mean(axis=-1)\n```\n\niii. The AI explains the 50 ms bin size as \"a compromise between the reference decoding bin (75 ms) and temporal resolution for time-varying outputs, and it puts the go cue exactly on a bin edge.\"\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. This is a synthetic variable defined by the time axis. It equals the bin centres of the 50 ms output bins, spanning approximately -2.475 to +2.475 s.\n\nii.\n```python\nedges = np.round(np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + 1e-9, DT_FINE), 6)\ncentres = edges[:-1] + DT_FINE / 2.0\n...\ncentres_out = centres[:nout * DOWNSAMPLE].reshape(nout, DOWNSAMPLE).mean(axis=1)\n...\ntin = TOUT.astype(np.float32)[None, :]\ninputs = [tin.copy() for _ in range(kt.size)]\n```\n\niii. N/A - this is defined by the decoder specification.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds*?\n\ni. The bin centres are computed once from the time axis parameters. No processing of raw data is needed; this variable is the shared time grid for all trials.\n\nii. Same as 3-a above.\n\niii. N/A.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from go cue onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The input is the bin centres of the same grid used for neural data, so alignment is automatic by construction.\n\nii.\n```python\nEDGES, TCENT, TOUT = time_axes()\n# TOUT is used as both the input variable and defines the neural time axis\n```\n\niii. N/A.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Three per-trial fields: `bp.R` (right-instructed), `bp.hit`, and `bp.miss`. The actual lick direction is inferred from the combination of instructed side and outcome.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = np.nan_to_num(bp['hit']).astype(bool)\nmiss = np.nan_to_num(bp['miss']).astype(bool)\nR = np.nan_to_num(bp['R']).astype(bool)\nL = np.nan_to_num(bp['L']).astype(bool)\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"Verified in data: identical to the direction of the first lickport contact after the go cue on 382/382 trials of JEB6_2021-04-18.\"\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction*?\n\ni. A hit on a right-instructed trial means the animal licked right; a miss on a right-instructed trial means it licked left (and vice versa). Ignore trials get class 2 (none). Codes: left=0, right=1, none=2. The value is constant across all time bins within a trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nlick_dir = np.full(kt.size, 2, dtype=np.int64)          # 2 = none\nright_lick = (R[kt] & hit[kt]) | (L[kt] & miss[kt])\nleft_lick = (L[kt] & hit[kt]) | (R[kt] & miss[kt])\nlick_dir[right_lick] = 1\nlick_dir[left_lick] = 0\n```\n\niii. Matches `getPrevChoice.m`: `choice = (R&hit)|(L&miss)`.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Behavioral context* derived from?\n\ni. `bp.autowater`: 1 = water-cued (WC) trial, 0 = delayed-response (DR) trial.\n\nii.\n```python\naw = np.nan_to_num(bp['autowater']).astype(bool)\ncontext = np.where(aw[kt], 0, 1).astype(np.int64)       # 0 = WC, 1 = DR\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"`autowater` used 'as a proxy for obtaining water-cued blocks and delayed-response blocks' (WorkingWithDataObjs.m)\".\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Behavioral context*?\n\ni. Direct mapping: autowater=1 -> WC (0), autowater=0 -> DR (1). The value is constant across all time bins. No further processing.\n\nii. Same as 5-a above.\n\niii. Matches the reference code's condition strings.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. `bp.hit` and `bp.miss`. Ignore trials are identified as those that are neither hit nor miss.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = np.nan_to_num(bp['hit']).astype(bool)\nmiss = np.nan_to_num(bp['miss']).astype(bool)\noutcome = np.full(kt.size, 2, dtype=np.int64)           # 2 = ignore\noutcome[hit[kt]] = 1\noutcome[miss[kt]] = 0\n```\n\niii. From CONVERSION_NOTES: \"outcome = bp.hit, with bp.no (ignore) trials set to NaN\" in the reference `getOutcome.m`; here ignore trials are kept as class 2.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. Direct relabelling: miss -> incorrect (0), hit -> correct (1), neither -> ignore (2). The value is constant across all time bins within a trial.\n\nii. Same as 6-a above.\n\niii. Matches the decoder specification ordering.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue velocity* derived from?\n\ni. DeepLabCut tracking from the **side camera only**: feature `tongue` in `obj.traj{1}`. The tracked x, y coordinates and frame times are extracted. `bp.ev.goCue` and `sglx.bitcode.bitstart` are used for clock alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\nTONGUE_FEAT = ('side', 'tongue')\n...\ntongue_sp, _ = feature_speed(sess, TONGUE_FEAT, kt, vidshift, align, fill_missing=False)\n```\n\niii. The AI documents: \"The tongue, jaw and nose were tracked using both cameras, whereas the paws were tracked using only the bottom view\" (paper quote), but only extracts the side camera tongue feature. The bottom camera's `top_tongue` feature is not used for tongue velocity.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. (1) Position is interpolated from frame times onto the 10 ms fine grid using `np.interp`. (2) NaN-marked frames (DLC not visible) remain NaN (no filling, matching the reference `findPosition.m` exception for tongue). (3) Velocity is computed as `np.gradient` of the interpolated position. (4) Speed = `np.hypot(vx, vy)`. (5) Non-visible bins remain NaN. (6) Speed is downsampled to 50 ms bins using `nanmean`. (7) Discretized at the per-session 50th percentile; not-visible bins -> class 2.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef feature_speed(sess, key, keep_trials, vidshift, align_times, fill_missing):\n    ...\n    pos = np.empty((NFINE, 2))\n    for d in range(2):\n        pos[:, d] = np.interp(TCENT, ft[valid], xy[valid, d], left=np.nan, right=np.nan)\n    vis = np.isfinite(pos).all(axis=1)\n    ...\n    vel = np.gradient(pos, axis=0)\n    sp = np.hypot(vel[:, 0], vel[:, 1])\n    sp[~vis] = np.nan\n    ...\ntongue_ds = downsample_mean(tongue_sp, nanmean=True)\ntongue_cls, tongue_thr = discretize(tongue_ds)\n```\n\niii. Follows `findPosition.m` (interp1 onto time axis) and `findVelocity.m` (gradient). Tongue NaNs are preserved as in the reference. No normalization since only one camera view is used.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session 50th percentile of all finite (visible) tongue speed values across all trials and time bins. Below threshold = 0, at or above = 1, not visible (NaN) = 2.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize(values, nan_class=2):\n    finite = np.isfinite(values)\n    cls = np.full(values.shape, nan_class, dtype=np.int64)\n    if finite.any():\n        thr = np.percentile(values[finite], 50)\n        cls[finite & (values >= thr)] = 1\n        cls[finite & (values < thr)] = 0\n    ...\n    return cls, thr\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder specification: \"0: < 50th percentile, 1: >= 50th percentile, 2: not visible\".\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Paw velocity*?\n\ni. Same interpolation pipeline as tongue velocity, but with `fill_missing=True`: (1) Interpolate position onto 10 ms grid, (2) nearest-neighbor fill for missing values (matching `fillmissing('nearest')` in `findPosition.m`), (3) gradient of position, (4) baseline derivative subtraction (median of diff, matching `findVelocity.m`), (5) speed = hypot, (6) non-visible bins set to NaN, (7) average both paw speeds, (8) downsample to 50 ms bins, (9) discretize at session 50th percentile.\n\nii.\n```python\n# In feature_speed with fill_missing=True:\nif fill_missing:\n    if vis.any():\n        idx = np.arange(NFINE)\n        pos = np.stack([np.interp(idx, idx[vis], pos[vis, d]) for d in range(2)], axis=1)\nvel = np.gradient(pos, axis=0)\nif fill_missing:\n    base = np.nanmedian(np.diff(pos, axis=0), axis=0)\n    vel = vel - base[0]\n```\n\niii.
